1	# iiac-perf
2	
3	Measure performance of various Inter-Intra Application Communication
4	(IIAC) techniques.
5	
6	## Design (0.2.0)
7	
8	Design a Rust app that can measure the cost of various (IIAC) techniques.
9	By IIAC I mean all communication techniques between and within
10	applications such as regular function calls vs async function calls vs
11	channels vs serde/deser of json or zero-copy messaging using io_uring and
12	potentially any other technique. It would include between threads or
13	processes or apps on the same computer or between apps on the same LAN or
14	between apps over a WAN.
15	
16	Ideally I'd like to be able to see a histogram of the range of times of the
17	send and receive costs, not just the mean/average of 10^3 or 10^6 invocations.
18	So the cost of the work should or could be something near-zero such as just
19	echoing the input, but maybe there is value in doing some constant but
20	variable amount of work to verify it's in-consequential. One thing I think we
21	need to be aware of is that if we make the work too simple the compiler could
22	just keep things in registers which would not be representative of "real" work.
23	
24	We should start simple, like comparing normal and async function calls and
25	later expand to other techniques.
26	
27	## Convention
28	
29	This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
30	a bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal
31	is that this main repo contains the "what", while the partner
32	bot repo contains "why" and "how". The key to the convention
33	is each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there
34	is a coherent story of the development of the project across time.
35	
36	The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
37	which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
38	first test bed.
39	
40	## Cloning
41	
42	Use [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1) to clone
43	the dual-repo project. It handles `git clone --recursive`,
44	`jj` init for both repos, and the Claude Code symlink:
45	
46	```
47	vc-x1 clone winksaville/iiac-perf
48	```
49	
50	## jj Tips for Git Users
51	
52	See [notes/jj-tips](notes/jj-tips.md)
53	
54	## License
55	
56	Licensed under either of
57	
58	- Apache License, Version 2.0 ([LICENSE-APACHE](LICENSE-APACHE) or http://apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0)
59	- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
60	
61	### Contribution
62	
63	Unless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted
64	for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall
65	be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.
66	
67	[1]: https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch
68	
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore. The section header is short and sweet
19	and the title is appended with the version number of the app when the chore
20	is completed.
21	
22	## Create an app that does something interesting (0.1.0)
23	
24	The app counts from 1 to 100, not to interesting.
25	```
26	
27	## jj tips
28	
29	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
30	
31	```
32	## Chores format
33	
34	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
35	example: chores-01.md
36	
37	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
38	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
39	
40	Example chore file:
41	```
42	# Chores-01.md
43	 
44	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
45	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
46	how and why of a particular chore.
47	
48	## Do something (1.3.1)
49	
50	Describe something
51	```
52	
53	## Versioning during development
54	
55	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
56	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
57	we go.
58	
59	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
60	
61	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
62	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
63	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
64	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
65	
66	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
67	
68	For multi-step:
69	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
70	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
71	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
72	
73	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
74	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
75	commits were exploratory vs final.
76	
77	## Todo format
78	
79	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
80	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
81	references.
82	
83	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
84	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
85	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
86	
87	Examples:
88	
89	# Todo
90	
91	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
92	- Fix bug Y [1]
93	
94	# Done
95	
96	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
97	
98	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
99	
```

1	use std::hint::black_box;
2	
3	use clap::Parser;
4	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
5	
6	const DEFAULT_ITERATIONS: u64 = 10_000_000;
7	const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;
8	const INNER_ITERATIONS: u64 = 10;
9	
10	#[derive(Parser)]
11	#[command(version, about = "Timer overhead measurement")]
12	struct Cli {
13	    /// Number of outer iterations
14	    #[arg(short, long, default_value_t = DEFAULT_ITERATIONS)]
15	    iterations: u64,
16	}
17	
18	fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {
19	    let s = n.to_string();
20	    let mut result = String::new();
21	    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {
22	        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {
23	            result.push(',');
24	        }
25	        result.push(c);
26	    }
27	    result.chars().rev().collect()
28	}
29	
30	fn round_elapsed_time(elapsed_ns: u64) -> u64 {
31	    (elapsed_ns + (INNER_ITERATIONS / 2)) / INNER_ITERATIONS
32	}
33	
34	fn measure_minstant(iterations: u64, hist: &mut Histogram<u64>) {
35	    // Warmup
36	    for _ in 0..WARMUP {
37	        black_box(minstant::Instant::now());
38	    }
39	
40	    // Measure
41	    for _ in 0..iterations {
42	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
43	        for _ in 0..INNER_ITERATIONS {
44	            black_box(minstant::Instant::now());
45	        }
46	        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
47	        hist.record(round_elapsed_time(elapsed_ns)).unwrap();
48	    }
49	}
50	
51	fn measure_instant(iterations: u64, hist: &mut Histogram<u64>) {
52	    // Warmup
53	    for _ in 0..WARMUP {
54	        black_box(std::time::Instant::now());
55	    }
56	
57	    // Measure using minstant as the reference timer
58	    for _ in 0..iterations {
59	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
60	        for _ in 0..INNER_ITERATIONS {
61	            black_box(std::time::Instant::now());
62	        }
63	        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
64	        hist.record(round_elapsed_time(elapsed_ns)).unwrap();
65	    }
66	}
67	
68	fn print_histogram(name: &str, iterations: u64, hist: &Histogram<u64>) {
69	    println!(
70	        "{name} ({} calls)",
71	        fmt_commas(iterations * INNER_ITERATIONS)
72	    );
73	    println!("  min      {:>10} ns", fmt_commas(hist.min()));
74	    println!(
75	        "  p1       {:>10} ns",
76	        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.01))
77	    );
78	    println!(
79	        "  p10      {:>10} ns",
80	        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.10))
81	    );
82	    println!(
83	        "  p50      {:>10} ns",
84	        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.50))
85	    );
86	    println!(
87	        "  p90      {:>10} ns",
88	        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.90))
89	    );
90	    println!(
91	        "  p99      {:>10} ns",
92	        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.99))
93	    );
94	    println!(
95	        "  p99.9    {:>10} ns",
96	        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.999))
97	    );
98	    println!(
99	        "  p99.99   {:>10} ns",
100	        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999))
101	    );
102	    println!("  max      {:>10} ns", fmt_commas(hist.max()));
103	    println!("  mean     {:>10.1} ns", hist.mean());
104	    println!("  stdev    {:>10.1} ns", hist.stdev());
105	    println!();
106	}
107	
108	fn main() {
109	    let cli = Cli::parse();
110	
111	    println!(
112	        "iiac-perf {} — timer overhead measurement\n",
113	        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")
114	    );
115	
116	    // 1ns to 1s range, 3 significant digits
117	    let mut minstant_hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();
118	    let mut instant_hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();
119	
120	    measure_minstant(cli.iterations, &mut minstant_hist);
121	    measure_instant(cli.iterations, &mut instant_hist);
122	
123	    print_histogram("minstant::Instant::now()", cli.iterations, &minstant_hist);
124	    print_histogram("std::time::Instant::now()", cli.iterations, &instant_hist);
125	}
126	
```

> AGENT

Acquainted. Here's the picture:

**Project**: `iiac-perf` v0.1.0 — Rust tool measuring performance of Inter/Intra-Application Communication techniques (function calls, async, channels, serde, io_uring, network, etc.). Uses `clap`, `hdrhistogram`, `minstant`.

**Current state**: Just shipped 0.1.0 (`feat: measure timer overhead`) — compares `minstant::Instant::now()` vs `std::time::Instant::now()` via histograms. Working tree clean.

**Next up (todo.md)**: "Design an app to measure IIAC performance written in Rust" — the core design work; timer overhead was a stepping stone.

**Conventions to follow**:
- Dual-repo: app at `.`, bot session at `.claude`, cross-linked via `ochid:` trailers
- Every change starts with a version bump (single-step vs multi-step, needs approval)
- Pre-commit: `cargo fmt` → `clippy` → `test` → `install` → retest → update `notes/todo.md`, `notes/chores-*.md`, `notes/README.md`
- Two-checkpoint session end: commit (approval) → push+finalize (approval); nothing after finalize
- No memory dir exists — per global CLAUDE.md, prefer repo-checked instructions anyway

Ready when you are.

> DEVELOPER

Ok, now for a "real" perf, I'd like to refactor so iiac-perf we can add other benches right now we have two and test both at the same time, but now I want a third, measure performance of channel then others. What would you suggest

> AGENT

I'd suggest a `Bench` trait (`name()`, `run(iterations) -> Histogram`) plus a registry `Vec<Box<dyn Bench>>`, driven by clap subcommands like `iiac-perf timer` / `iiac-perf channel` with an `all` default — each bench lives in its own module (`benches/timer.rs`, `benches/channel.rs`) and the shared histogram-printing + warmup/timing harness moves into a small `harness` module.

Tradeoff: the trait + registry is mild over-engineering for 2–3 benches today, but given the README's trajectory (async, serde, io_uring, LAN, WAN…) a flat `match` in `main` gets painful fast; going trait-based now means each future bench is a self-contained file instead of another branch added to a growing function.

One open question before I'd start: for `channel`, what do you want measured first — `std::sync::mpsc` round-trip latency on one thread, cross-thread send→recv, or something like `crossbeam`/`tokio::mpsc` too?

> DEVELOPER

let's start simple std:sync:spsc on one thread, but eventually lots of combinaions, I like the trait. Also, lets use minstant we've measure it to be faster, also I'd like to subtract the loop and timing overhead using the "min" time from minstant and also the cost of the loop by timing an empty loop, thoughts?

> AGENT

Sounds good, a few thoughts before we start:

**Nit**: `std::sync::mpsc` — there's no `spsc` in std, but using mpsc single-threaded is fine (and the most "apples-to-apples" baseline before we add `crossbeam`/`tokio`/etc).

**Overhead subtraction**: I'd calibrate two things in the harness and report both raw + adjusted:
- `timer_overhead` = min of back-to-back `Instant::now(); elapsed()` pairs — the floor of the measurement apparatus
- `loop_overhead` = per-iter cost of an empty `for _ in 0..N { black_box(()); }` loop

Then per-call = `(elapsed − timer_overhead) / N − loop_overhead`.

Caveat to flag: subtracting a *min* from a *distribution* is asymmetric — at p1 the adjusted value can go near zero / negative for benches close to the floor. I'd print raw and adjusted side-by-side rather than only adjusted, so nothing gets hidden.

**Structure** I'd propose:
```
src/
  main.rs              # CLI: `iiac-perf [timer|channel|all] -i N`
  harness.rs           # Bench trait + runner + calibration + histogram printer
  benches/
    mod.rs
    timer.rs           # minstant vs std::time::Instant
    channel.rs         # std::sync::mpsc, single thread
```

Trait roughly:
```rust
pub trait Bench {
    fn name(&self) -> &str;
    fn step(&mut self);   // one unit; harness times N per sample
}
```

**Version**: this is a refactor + new bench + calibration — enough to want intermediate commits. I'd suggest **multi-step 0.2.0-devN**: `-dev1` refactor to trait/harness (no behavior change), `-dev2` add calibration + adjusted output, `-dev3` add channel bench, then 0.2.0 finalize. Single-step is viable if you'd rather land it all at once — your call.

Want me to go with that layout and the multi-step plan?

> DEVELOPER

yes andadd the plan to chores + todo

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "This bootstraps the benchmarking project by measuring the measuring stick first,\nvalidating the measurement approach before applying it to actual IIAC techniques.",
  "new_string": "This bootstraps the benchmarking project by measuring the measuring stick first,\nvalidating the measurement approach before applying it to actual IIAC techniques.\n\n## Refactor to Bench trait + add channel bench (0.2.0)\n\nRestructure `iiac-perf` so new IIAC benchmarks can be added as self-contained\nmodules instead of hand-wiring each into `main`. Also add overhead calibration\nand the first real IIAC bench: `std::sync::mpsc` round-trip on one thread.\n\n### Why\n\nThe README's trajectory (async, serde, io_uring, LAN, WAN, …) implies many\nbenchmarks. A `match` in `main` would grow unwieldy. Calibrating timer and\nloop overhead lets each bench report a per-call cost closer to the technique\nitself rather than the measurement apparatus.\n\n### How\n\nMulti-step, target version `0.2.0`. Approach approved by user.\n\nPlanned module layout:\n\n```\nsrc/\n  main.rs              # CLI: `iiac-perf [timer|channel|all] -i N`\n  harness.rs           # Bench trait + runner + histogram printer\n  overhead.rs          # timer + loop overhead calibration\n  benches/\n    mod.rs\n    timer.rs           # minstant vs std::time::Instant\n    channel.rs         # std::sync::mpsc round trip, single thread\n```\n\nTrait sketch:\n\n```rust\npub trait Bench {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str;\n    fn step(&mut self);  // one unit of work; harness times N per sample\n}\n```\n\nOverhead subtraction:\n\n- `timer_overhead_ns` — min of back-to-back `Instant::now() / elapsed()` pairs\n- `loop_overhead_ns`  — per-iter cost of empty `for _ in 0..N { black_box(()); }`\n- `per_call = (elapsed - timer_overhead)/N - loop_overhead`\n\nPrint **raw and adjusted side-by-side**. Subtracting a min from a full\ndistribution is asymmetric — near the floor, adjusted percentiles can go\nnear zero. Showing both keeps the raw data honest.\n\n### Dev steps\n\n1. `0.2.0-dev1` — chore marker: bump version, write this plan, update todo.\n2. `0.2.0-dev2` — refactor: introduce `Bench` trait, `harness` module, move\n   timer measurement into `benches/timer.rs`. No behavior change beyond\n   structure.\n3. `0.2.0-dev3` — add timer + loop overhead calibration, print raw + adjusted.\n4. `0.2.0-dev4` — add `benches/channel.rs` (`std::sync::mpsc`, single thread)\n   + CLI subcommand dispatch.\n5. `0.2.0` — finalize: drop `-devN`, update todo/chores Done."
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "content": "use hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\npub const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;\npub const INNER_ITERATIONS: u64 = 10;\n\npub trait Bench {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str;\n    fn step(&mut self);\n}\n\npub fn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64) -> Histogram<u64> {\n    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();\n\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        bench.step();\n    }\n\n    for _ in 0..iterations {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..INNER_ITERATIONS {\n            bench.step();\n        }\n        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns)).unwrap();\n    }\n\n    hist\n}\n\nfn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64) -> u64 {\n    (elapsed_ns + (INNER_ITERATIONS / 2)) / INNER_ITERATIONS\n}\n\npub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {\n    let s = n.to_string();\n    let mut result = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            result.push(',');\n        }\n        result.push(c);\n    }\n    result.chars().rev().collect()\n}\n\npub fn print_histogram(name: &str, iterations: u64, hist: &Histogram<u64>) {\n    println!(\n        \"{name} ({} calls)\",\n        fmt_commas(iterations * INNER_ITERATIONS)\n    );\n    println!(\"  min      {:>10} ns\", fmt_commas(hist.min()));\n    println!(\n        \"  p1       {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.01))\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  p10      {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.10))\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  p50      {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.50))\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  p90      {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.90))\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  p99      {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.99))\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  p99.9    {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.999))\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  p99.99   {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999))\n    );\n    println!(\"  max      {:>10} ns\", fmt_commas(hist.max()));\n    println!(\"  mean     {:>10.1} ns\", hist.mean());\n    println!(\"  stdev    {:>10.1} ns\", hist.stdev());\n    println!();\n}\n"
}
```

{

  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench};\n\npub struct MinstantNow;\n\nimpl Bench for MinstantNow {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"minstant::Instant::now()\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) {\n        black_box(minstant::Instant::now());\n    }\n}\n\npub struct StdInstantNow;\n\nimpl Bench for StdInstantNow {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"std::time::Instant::now()\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) {\n        black_box(std::time::Instant::now());\n    }\n}\n\npub fn run(iterations: u64) {\n    let mut minstant_bench = MinstantNow;\n    let minstant_hist = harness::run_bench(&mut minstant_bench, iterations);\n    harness::print_histogram(minstant_bench.name(), iterations, &minstant_hist);\n\n    let mut std_bench = StdInstantNow;\n    let std_hist = harness::run_bench(&mut std_bench, iterations);\n    harness::print_histogram(std_bench.name(), iterations, &std_hist);\n}\n"
}

95	
96	### Versioning
97	
98	Every change must start with a version bump. See
99	[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
100	for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
101	
102	### Chores section headers
103	
104	Chores section headers use trailing version format:
105	
106	```
107	## Description (X.Y.Z)
108	```
109	
110	Example: `## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0)`
111	
112	### Pre-commit checklist
113	
114	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
115	
116	1. `cargo fmt`
117	2. `cargo clippy`
118	3. `cargo test`
119	4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
120	5. Retest after install
121	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
122	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
123	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
124	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
125	
126	## ochid Trailers
127	
128	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
129	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
130	path followed by the changeID:
131	
132	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
133	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
134	
135	Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
136	is app repo, second is `.claude`).

I think they'll be a need for a common.rs with function all benches can use, correct?

> AGENT

Probably yes eventually — but I'd hold off until dev4. Right now `harness.rs` already holds the shared timing/histogram code, and `timer.rs` has no payload or message types to share with anything. Adding `common.rs` now would just be an empty module.

The natural moment is when `channel.rs` lands: if it introduces a message type, a work-simulation helper, or a round-trip pattern that a second channel bench (crossbeam, tokio::mpsc) will also want, extract then. Rule of three — extract on the second consumer, not speculatively on the first.

Sound reasonable, or do you want it stubbed now?

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse crate::harness::{INNER_ITERATIONS, WARMUP};\n\npub struct Overhead {\n    pub timer_ns: u64,\n    pub loop_iter_ns: u64,\n}\n\nimpl Overhead {\n    pub fn per_call_ns(&self) -> u64 {\n        self.timer_ns / INNER_ITERATIONS + self.loop_iter_ns\n    }\n}\n\npub fn calibrate(samples: u64) -> Overhead {\n    let timer_ns = calibrate_timer(samples);\n    let loop_iter_ns = calibrate_loop(samples, timer_ns);\n    Overhead {\n        timer_ns,\n        loop_iter_ns,\n    }\n}\n\nfn calibrate_timer(samples: u64) -> u64 {\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        let s = minstant::Instant::now();\n        black_box(s.elapsed().as_nanos());\n    }\n    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..samples {\n        let s = minstant::Instant::now();\n        let e = s.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        if e < min_ns {\n            min_ns = e;\n        }\n    }\n    min_ns\n}\n\nfn calibrate_loop(samples: u64, timer_ns: u64) -> u64 {\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        let s = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..INNER_ITERATIONS {\n            black_box(());\n        }\n        black_box(s.elapsed().as_nanos());\n    }\n    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..samples {\n        let s = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..INNER_ITERATIONS {\n            black_box(());\n        }\n        let e = s.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        if e < min_ns {\n            min_ns = e;\n        }\n    }\n    min_ns.saturating_sub(timer_ns) / INNER_ITERATIONS\n}\n"
}
```

{

  "content": "use hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::overhead::Overhead;\n\npub const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;\npub const INNER_ITERATIONS: u64 = 10;\n\npub trait Bench {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str;\n    fn step(&mut self);\n}\n\npub fn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64) -> Histogram<u64> {\n    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();\n\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        bench.step();\n    }\n\n    for _ in 0..iterations {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..INNER_ITERATIONS {\n            bench.step();\n        }\n        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns)).unwrap();\n    }\n\n    hist\n}\n\nfn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64) -> u64 {\n    (elapsed_ns + (INNER_ITERATIONS / 2)) / INNER_ITERATIONS\n}\n\npub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {\n    let s = n.to_string();\n    let mut result = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            result.push(',');\n        }\n        result.push(c);\n    }\n    result.chars().rev().collect()\n}\n\npub fn print_histogram(\n    name: &str,\n    iterations: u64,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    overhead: &Overhead,\n) {\n    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns();\n    println!(\n        \"{name} ({} calls)\",\n        fmt_commas(iterations * INNER_ITERATIONS)\n    );\n    println!(\"                      raw      adjusted\");\n    print_row(\"min\", hist.min(), adj);\n    print_row(\"p1\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.01), adj);\n    print_row(\"p10\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.10), adj);\n    print_row(\"p50\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.50), adj);\n    print_row(\"p90\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.90), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.99), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99.9\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99.99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999), adj);\n    print_row(\"max\", hist.max(), adj);\n    let mean = hist.mean();\n    let adj_mean = (mean - adj as f64).max(0.0);\n    println!(\"  mean       {:>10.1} ns    {:>10.1} ns\", mean, adj_mean);\n    println!(\"  stdev      {:>10.1} ns\", hist.stdev());\n    println!();\n}\n\nfn print_row(label: &str, raw: u64, overhead: u64) {\n    let adj = raw.saturating_sub(overhead);\n    println!(\n        \"  {label:<8} {:>10} ns    {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(raw),\n        fmt_commas(adj)\n    );\n}\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Before I commit dev3, I'm seeing calibration coming out as 0 ns and it made me rethink the approach. Quick question.

Smoke test output:
```
calibration (min over 100,000 samples):
  timer_overhead       0 ns  (per Instant::now+elapsed pair)
  loop_iter_cost       0 ns  (per empty loop iteration)
```

The 0s aren't bugs — they're real: TSC-based `minstant::now()` pairs pipeline, so back-to-back reads observe sub-ns deltas that round to 0. That means the *isolated* timer/loop measurements don't reflect what actually gets baked into a harness sample.

What the harness sample actually contains is: `(2 now() calls) + (N loop iterations with step)`. The cleanest "apparatus cost per recorded unit" is: **run the harness against an empty `Bench` and take its min**. That single number is exactly what should be subtracted from real benches — it naturally includes both timer framing (divided by N) and per-iter loop cost.

I'd switch to:
- Report `timer_pair_ns` (your original min-of-minstant request, informational — shows timer granularity)
- Report `empty_call_ns` = min from `EmptyBench` run through `run_bench` (this is what gets subtracted)
- Adjusted column = `raw - empty_call_ns`

Same signal you asked for, but the subtracted value reflects actual apparatus cost instead of a pipelined 0. OK to switch, or keep as-is and accept that adjusted ≈ raw for fast timers?

> DEVELOPER

our measurments from the original code showed 9ns minimum why 0 now?

> AGENT

Different things being measured.

**Original 9ns** = `min` of a histogram where each sample is `(2 framing now() calls) + (10 inner now() calls in a busy loop with black_box)`, divided by 10. The inner now()s can't pipeline freely — each is separated by black_box + loop branch — so each pays close to its real cost. 12 calls / 10 records ≈ 1.2× per-call ≈ 9 ns ⟹ true now() ≈ 7–8 ns.

**My calibrate_timer 0ns** = min of `Instant::now(); s.elapsed()` back-to-back with **nothing between them**. The CPU's out-of-order engine pipelines both TSC reads — the second one is in flight before the first retires — so observed delta rounds to 0 with TSC granularity.

Both are "true," but they measure different regimes. The 9ns is what real benches actually pay; the 0ns is a lower bound when the CPU is idle. That's exactly why subtracting 0 doesn't help — it's not the apparatus cost a real bench experiences.

The EmptyBench-through-`run_bench` approach measures apparatus cost *in the same regime* as real benches (same framing, same loop, same scheduling pressure), so the subtraction is apples-to-apples. That's why I'd switch.

> DEVELOPER

I say timer_pair_ns but i'd like to subtract the loop costs too, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good — that's exactly what `empty_call_ns` does, since it's measured *through the same harness*: a sample of EmptyBench includes both the timer framing (the start `now()` and the `elapsed()` call) AND the N empty loop iterations. Its min, when divided to per-call, is `(timer_pair / N) + loop_per_iter` — both costs in one number, measured in the same regime as real benches.

Proposed output:
```
calibration:
  timer_pair         0 ns  (raw back-to-back now+elapsed; informational only)
  apparatus/call     X ns  (subtracted from adjusted column)
                          = timer_framing/N + per-iter loop cost,
                            measured by running EmptyBench through the harness
```

Adjusted column = `raw - apparatus_per_call`. Sound right?

> DEVELOPER

Above you said "Report timer_pair_ns (your original min-of-minstant ..)" which is on the order of 9ns on my dev computer, 3900x, but then in "Proposed output:" you say "timer_par 0 ns" and "apparatus/call X ns" so I'm confused.

> AGENT

You're right, my fault — I muddled two different "min"s.

**The 9 ns** is the min of the *minstant timer bench histogram* — i.e., the per-call cost of `Instant::now()` when called 10 times inside the framed loop. That's the workload of the timer bench itself.

**The 0 ns** was my `calibrate_timer`, which measured `Instant::now() + s.elapsed()` back-to-back with **nothing between** — those pipeline on TSC and the delta rounds to 0. Different measurement.

So `timer_pair` would in fact print 0 ns on your 3900x, not 9 ns — and that's exactly why it's misleading as a subtraction value, which is what I was trying to flag.

Two reasonable paths:

**Option A** (my recommendation): drop `timer_pair` entirely. Report one number — `apparatus_per_call` from running EmptyBench through the harness (≈ 1–2 ns: framing 2 now()s / 10 + per-iter loop). Subtract that. Clean, no confusing 0.

**Option B**: keep `timer_pair` but label it honestly as "pipelined floor — measures TSC granularity, not subtractable cost," and still subtract `apparatus_per_call`.

Want A or B?

> DEVELOPER

One nummber is simpler and these aren't gospel, but I'd like them to be "as-good-as-possible" so I like "apparatus/call" being "measured by running EmptyBench through the harness". So let's see what the results look like after 0.2.0-dev3 is complete and see how it looks

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse crate::harness::{Bench, INNER_ITERATIONS, WARMUP};\n\npub struct Overhead {\n    pub per_sample_min_ns: u64,\n}\n\nimpl Overhead {\n    pub fn per_call_ns(&self) -> f64 {\n        self.per_sample_min_ns as f64 / INNER_ITERATIONS as f64\n    }\n}\n\nstruct EmptyBench;\n\nimpl Bench for EmptyBench {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"empty\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) {\n        black_box(());\n    }\n}\n\npub fn calibrate(samples: u64) -> Overhead {\n    let mut bench = EmptyBench;\n\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        bench.step();\n    }\n\n    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..samples {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..INNER_ITERATIONS {\n            bench.step();\n        }\n        let e = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        if e < min_ns {\n            min_ns = e;\n        }\n    }\n    Overhead {\n        per_sample_min_ns: min_ns,\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "new_string": "pub fn print_histogram(name: &str, iterations: u64, hist: &Histogram<u64>, overhead: &Overhead) {\n    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns();\n    println!(\n        \"{name} ({} calls)\",\n        fmt_commas(iterations * INNER_ITERATIONS)\n    );\n    println!(\"                      raw      adjusted\");\n    print_row(\"min\", hist.min(), adj);\n    print_row(\"p1\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.01), adj);\n    print_row(\"p10\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.10), adj);\n    print_row(\"p50\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.50), adj);\n    print_row(\"p90\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.90), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.99), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99.9\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99.99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999), adj);\n    print_row(\"max\", hist.max(), adj);\n    let mean = hist.mean();\n    let adj_mean = (mean - adj).max(0.0);\n    println!(\"  mean       {:>10.1} ns    {:>10.1} ns\", mean, adj_mean);\n    println!(\"  stdev      {:>10.1} ns\", hist.stdev());\n    println!();\n}\n\nfn print_row(label: &str, raw: u64, overhead: f64) {\n    let adj = (raw as f64 - overhead).max(0.0);\n    println!(\n        \"  {label:<8} {:>10} ns    {:>10.1} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(raw),\n        adj\n    );\n}"

> AGENT

Empty-bench per-sample also comes out 0 ns. The reason runs deeper than rounding:

When the inner loop is empty (`black_box(())` is essentially free), there's nothing serial between the two `now()` calls, so the CPU pipelines them and the TSC delta between the framing `Instant::now()` and the `Instant::now()` inside `elapsed()` is below TSC granularity → returns 0.

This is fundamental on TSC + out-of-order CPUs: **apparatus cost only manifests when something is happening between the now()s**. As soon as something is — like a real `step()` — that something dominates and apparatus contribution is negligible.

So the honest result of the calibration: apparatus is sub-ns and not subtractable. The "adjusted" column ≈ raw column. Real numbers from your 3900x:

```
calibration (100,000 empty-bench samples):
  apparatus/sample      0 ns  (timer framing + 10 empty loop iters)
  apparatus/call      0.0 ns  (subtracted from adjusted column)

minstant: min 9 ns, p99 11 ns, max ~2 µs (jitter)
std::time: min 23 ns, p99 27 ns, max ~2.5 µs
```

Three options for dev3:

**A.** Ship as-is — calibration is honest, apparatus = 0 is a real finding, adjusted column stays (it'll matter if/when we add a slower timer impl or run on a non-TSC clock source).

**B.** Drop the adjusted column entirely, just print a one-line note: "apparatus < 1 ns on TSC clocks; not subtracted." Less noise.

**C.** Skip dev3 calibration, go straight to dev4 (channel bench). Calibration revisited later when we have a bench where apparatus might actually matter.

Which?

> AGENT

Same 0 ns result. Reason: `bench.step()` returns `()`, so `black_box(bench.step())` is `black_box(())` — a no-op around a no-op. EmptyBench's body itself is already `black_box(())`. Wrapping again doesn't introduce a serial dependency.

The deeper fact: there's nothing at all between the two TSC reads in the framed region. The compiler inlines `EmptyBench::step` to nothing, and the CPU's OoO engine pipelines both `now()` calls — observed delta = 0. No `black_box` placement around an empty step can change that.

To force a non-zero apparatus reading we'd need actual serial work between the now()s — which means it's no longer pure apparatus, it's "apparatus + the work we added."

So I think the conclusion stands: on TSC + OoO, pure apparatus cost is sub-resolution, and any subtraction baseline we'd compute would either be 0 (truthful but useless) or contaminated by inserted work (subtracts more than apparatus, biasing real benches low).

Suggestion: ship dev3 with calibration as a transparent diagnostic (prints "apparatus/sample 0 ns — pipelines below TSC granularity"), keep the adjusted column for the eventual case where it matters (a slow clock source, or future cross-process benches where syscall overhead dominates framing). Drop the misleading subtraction-by-zero theatre by only printing the "adjusted" column when apparatus > 0.

Or just rip the adjusted column out for now and add it back when there's a bench where it matters. Your call.

> AGENT

Still 0 ns. Same root cause: `bench.step()` returns `()`. `black_box(())` doesn't introduce a serial dependency because `()` is a zero-sized type — there's nothing for the compiler to "hold onto." Even with 5× `black_box(bench.step())` per iter (50 total per sample), the compiler still elides everything between the two `now()` reads.

To actually force work, black_box needs to operate on a real value. Two ways that would change the picture:

**1.** Force the receiver to be opaque so the compiler can't inline through the trait call:
```rust
black_box(&mut bench).step();
```

**2.** Have step return something non-trivial (e.g., `u64`) and black_box that:
```rust
trait Bench { fn step(&mut self) -> u64; }
// EmptyBench returns a counter
black_box(bench.step());  // now black_box has a real value
```

Want to try option 1 (smaller change, doesn't touch the trait), or option 2 (deeper but more bulletproof)?

> AGENT

Now non-zero — `step()` returning `u32` and `black_box`-ed at the call site forces serial work. But there's a now-inconsistency: calibrate does **5× step** per inner iter (50 per sample), while `run_bench` does **1× step** per inner iter (10 per sample). The label says "10 empty loop iters" but it's really 50, and `per_call_ns` divides by 10 — so reported 3.0 ns/call is actually 0.6 ns/call.

For the subtraction to be apples-to-apples with real benches, calibrate must mirror `run_bench`. Reverting calibrate to 1× step and also wrapping `run_bench`'s step call in `black_box` so the u32 is consumed there too.

> AGENT

Apples-to-apples (1× step per inner iter) → back to 0 ns. The 30 ns we saw earlier was 50 step calls / sample, so per-call ≈ 0.6 ns. With only 10 calls per sample that 6 ns total still pipelines below TSC resolution → 0.

So the picture is consistent: when the framed work is small (≤ ~10 trivial ops), TSC delta on this CPU rounds to 0. Three knobs we could turn:

1. **Bump INNER_ITERATIONS** (e.g. 100): apparatus measurement becomes non-zero and per-call value is meaningful — but real benches then average over more calls, losing some sample-to-sample variance signal.
2. **Calibrate with N_calibration ≠ INNER_ITERATIONS**: e.g. measure apparatus with 100 step calls per sample, divide by 100 → stable per-call number; real benches keep N=10. Subtraction stays valid since per-call cost is what we subtract.
3. **Accept 0** and ship as-is: subtraction is honest (apparatus < TSC res for INNER=10), the column just always equals raw on this hardware. Useful diagnostic for slower clock sources later.

I'd lean **#2** — gives a real apparatus number without changing the bench loop. What do you want?

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse crate::harness::{Bench, CALIBRATION_INNER, WARMUP};\n\npub struct Overhead {\n    pub per_sample_min_ns: u64,\n    pub calls_per_sample: u64,\n}\n\nimpl Overhead {\n    pub fn per_call_ns(&self) -> f64 {\n        self.per_sample_min_ns as f64 / self.calls_per_sample as f64\n    }\n}\n\nstruct EmptyBench;\n\nimpl Bench for EmptyBench {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"empty\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u32 {\n        black_box(1)\n    }\n}\n\npub fn calibrate(samples: u64) -> Overhead {\n    let mut bench = EmptyBench;\n\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..samples {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..CALIBRATION_INNER {\n            black_box(bench.step());\n        }\n        let e = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        if e < min_ns {\n            min_ns = e;\n        }\n    }\n    Overhead {\n        per_sample_min_ns: min_ns,\n        calls_per_sample: CALIBRATION_INNER,\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Working — apparatus/call = 0.60 ns, adjusted column now visibly differs from raw (minstant min 9 → 8.4, std min 23 → 22.4).

One caveat to flag, then your call on locking dev3:

The 0.60 ns is "framing/100 + per-iter empty cost." Most of it is per-iter cost (since 100 black_box(1)s dominate the 2 framing now() reads). When applied to real benches with N=10, the per-call framing share is actually 10× bigger (framing is per-sample, not per-call) — so we slightly **under-subtract** the framing portion for real benches. With framing ~15 ns: real per-call framing ≈ 1.5 ns vs the ~0.15 ns the subtraction accounts for. Off by ~1 ns at the per-call level.

To be precise we'd split into `framing_per_sample` + `loop_per_iter` and apply `adj = raw - framing/INNER - loop_per_iter`. That gets us back to two numbers.

Three options:
- **A.** Lock as-is (single number, ~1 ns under-subtract is honest noise floor anyway)
- **B.** Split into framing + per-iter, more precise subtraction
- **C.** Bump `INNER_ITERATIONS` so framing-per-call also shrinks — keeps single-number subtraction accurate

Lock A and move to dev4, or want to refine?

> DEVELOPER

Agree, A and move on

Single\n   `apparatus/call` number subtracted from each raw value to produce the\n   adjusted column. Notes from the experiment:\n   - First attempt with INNER=10 + `step()->()` produced 0 ns: TSC reads\n     pipeline when there's nothing serial between them.\n   - `black_box(())` doesn't introduce a serial dependency (ZST has\n     nothing to hold). Required `step()->u32` so `black_box(value)`\n     anchors the work.\n   - With matching N=10, even with non-trivial step return, total\n     framed work (~6 ns) still rounds to 0 under TSC granularity.\n   - Decoupling calibration N (=100) from harness N (=10) gives a\n     measurable per-call number (~0.6 ns) without changing the bench\n     loop. Slight under-subtract of framing-per-call (~1 ns) accepted\n     as honest noise floor; revisit if/when a bench needs precision."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {\n    let s = n.to_string();\n    let mut result = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            result.push(',');\n        }\n        result.push(c);\n    }\n    result.chars().rev().collect()\n}",
  "new_string": "pub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {\n    let s = n.to_string();\n    let mut result = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            result.push(',');\n        }\n        result.push(c);\n    }\n    result.chars().rev().collect()\n}\n\npub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {\n    let s = format!(\"{n:.decimals$}\");\n    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {\n        Some(rest) => (\"-\", rest),\n        None => (\"\", s.as_str()),\n    };\n    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {\n        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),\n        None => (body, \"\"),\n    };\n    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0);\n    format!(\"{sign}{}{frac_part}\", fmt_commas(int_num))\n}"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/channel.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::sync::mpsc;\n\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench};\nuse crate::overhead::Overhead;\n\npub struct StdMpscRoundTrip {\n    tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,\n    rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl StdMpscRoundTrip {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();\n        Self { tx, rx, counter: 0 }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for StdMpscRoundTrip {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"std::sync::mpsc round-trip (1 thread)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u32 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        self.tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();\n        let v = self.rx.recv().unwrap();\n        black_box(v) as u32\n    }\n}\n\npub fn run(iterations: u64, overhead: &Overhead) {\n    let mut bench = StdMpscRoundTrip::new();\n    let hist = harness::run_bench(&mut bench, iterations);\n    harness::print_histogram(bench.name(), iterations, &hist, overhead);\n}\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/main.rs",
  "content": "mod benches;\nmod harness;\nmod overhead;\n\nuse clap::{Parser, Subcommand};\n\nconst DEFAULT_ITERATIONS: u64 = 10_000_000;\nconst CALIBRATION_SAMPLES: u64 = 100_000;\n\n#[derive(Parser)]\n#[command(version, about = \"IIAC performance measurement\")]\nstruct Cli {\n    #[command(subcommand)]\n    command: Option<Command>,\n\n    /// Number of outer iterations\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value_t = DEFAULT_ITERATIONS, global = true)]\n    iterations: u64,\n}\n\n#[derive(Subcommand)]\nenum Command {\n    /// Run timer overhead benches (minstant vs std::time::Instant)\n    Timer,\n    /// Run channel benches (std::sync::mpsc round-trip, single thread)\n    Channel,\n    /// Run all benches (default)\n    All,\n}\n\nfn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n    let cmd = cli.command.unwrap_or(Command::All);\n\n    println!(\n        \"iiac-perf {} — IIAC performance measurement\\n\",\n        env!(\"CARGO_PKG_VERSION\")\n    );\n\n    let overhead = overhead::calibrate(CALIBRATION_SAMPLES);\n    println!(\n        \"calibration ({} empty-bench samples):\",\n        harness::fmt_commas(CALIBRATION_SAMPLES)\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  apparatus/sample  {:>7} ns  (timer framing + {} empty loop iters)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas(overhead.per_sample_min_ns),\n        overhead.calls_per_sample\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  apparatus/call    {:>7} ns  (subtracted from adjusted column)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.per_call_ns(), 2)\n    );\n    println!();\n\n    match cmd {\n        Command::Timer => benches::timer::run(cli.iterations, &overhead),\n        Command::Channel => benches::channel::run(cli.iterations, &overhead),\n        Command::All => {\n            benches::timer::run(cli.iterations, &overhead);\n            benches::channel::run(cli.iterations, &overhead);\n        }\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Yeah, std::sync::mpsc was rewritten around Rust 1.67 to use crossbeam-channel internally — `crossbeam-channel 0.5.15` is in your Cargo.lock pulled in via std. So you're effectively measuring crossbeam through the std API. Pure single-thread send+recv with no contention is just a couple of atomics, an allocation reuse, and queue head/tail bumps — all L1, ~60 cycles at 3.8 GHz tracks.

Worth noting: the moment we add a second thread (next bench candidate), the cache-line ping-pong between cores will jump these numbers by an order of magnitude or two.

Still need your approval on the dev4 commits from my prior message — should I proceed?

> DEVELOPER

Should we have step return a u64, down side would not nice to u32 CPUS

> AGENT

Good question. The "u32 CPU" downside is real but tiny for our case: `step` only needs to anchor a value through `black_box` — there's no arithmetic on the return value. On a 32-bit CPU, `black_box(u64)` is two register moves vs one for u32, but it's not in a tight inner loop that does math on it. Cost ≈ 1 extra instruction per call, ~0.3 ns on a 32-bit ARM. Negligible.

The upside of `u64`: drops the awkward `as u32` truncation in `channel.rs` (where the counter and message are already `u64`), and any future bench dealing with pointers/timestamps/sizes naturally returns `u64`. Less casting friction.

I'd switch to `u64`. Want me to fold it into dev4 (small change: trait + `EmptyBench` + 3 step impls + drop the `as u32`), or keep dev4 as-is and do it as a quick `dev5`/separate fix?

> DEVELOPER

y

- **Function-call baselines**: regular vs `Box<dyn Trait>` vs `async fn` (poll-once) — gives a "free side" of the spectrum to anchor the channel/serde numbers against.

> DEVELOPER

LG, let's add multi-threads version of channel as the next obvious test and add the other suggestions in your list to todo

> AGENT

For the multi-thread channel bench: I'd model it as a round-trip (apples-to-apples with the single-thread one) — main thread sends on channel A, worker thread recvs from A and sends back on channel B, main recvs from B. `step` measures full round-trip across the cores. Bench owns the worker thread + clean shutdown via dropping its sender on bench Drop.

Display names disambiguate: existing `"std::sync::mpsc round-trip (1 thread)"`, new `"std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads)"`.

Versioning — recommend **single-step to 0.3.0**: scope is one new bench file + a few todo additions, no architectural changes. Multi-step would just be ceremony. Sound good, or want multi-step?

> DEVELOPER

SG, maybe a pre-step is let's split timer into std-now and min-now or some such

> AGENT

Good idea. To pin the design before I start:

**File split** — proposed:
- `benches/min_now.rs` — `MinstantNow`
- `benches/std_now.rs` — `StdInstantNow`
- Delete `benches/timer.rs`

**CLI**: keep a `timer` subcommand that runs both, or expose `min-now` / `std-now` individually too? Simplest is keep `timer` as the umbrella (no new subcommands) — each bench file just exposes its `Bench` impl + a small `run` fn, and `Command::Timer` calls both. If you want individual subcommands I'd add them under `timer` as a sub-sub group, but that's noise unless you actually want to run them independently.

**Versioning**: since you called it a "pre-step," multi-step makes sense:
- `0.3.0-dev1` — split timer files (mechanical refactor, no behavior change)
- `0.3.0-dev2` — multi-thread channel bench + add suggestions to todo
- `0.3.0` — finalize

Confirm the file split + "keep `timer` umbrella, no new CLI" + multi-step, then I'll start dev1.

> DEVELOPER

I'd suggest allow a list of name and if someone want's both they mention both?

> AGENT

Got it — list of bench names as positional args. Concrete proposal:

```
iiac-perf [BENCH...] [-i N]

# examples
iiac-perf                       # default: all
iiac-perf all
iiac-perf min-now
iiac-perf min-now std-now
iiac-perf mpsc-1t mpsc-2t
```

Each bench module exposes `pub const NAME: &str` + `pub fn run(...)`. `main.rs` holds a registry slice `&[(name, fn)]` and dispatches; unknown names get a friendly error listing valid ones.

Initial names (dev1):
- `min-now` (MinstantNow)
- `std-now` (StdInstantNow)
- `mpsc-1t` (existing single-thread, renamed from `channel`)

Then dev2 adds `mpsc-2t`.

Two open questions:
1. Naming pattern — `mpsc-1t` / `mpsc-2t` (concise, scales) or `mpsc-same-thread` / `mpsc-cross-thread` (readable)?
2. The existing CLI uses `channel` → would become `mpsc-1t` (breaking but only a 0.x release; semver gives us free reign). OK to break?

Confirm naming + breaking change OK + multi-step plan, and I'll start dev1.

> DEVELOPER

Yep and 3 step it as you proposed

> AGENT

Starting dev1. Bumping version, writing chore plan, splitting bench files, registry-based CLI.

  "new_string": "5. `0.2.0` ✅ finalize: drop `-devN`, move todo entry to Done.\n\n## Multi-thread mpsc + per-bench files + named CLI (0.3.0)\n\nAdd the next obvious IIAC bench (cross-thread mpsc round-trip) and\nrestructure so each bench impl lives in its own file. Replace the\nfixed `timer`/`channel`/`all` subcommands with a positional list of\nbench names dispatched via a registry — composes naturally as more\nbenches arrive.\n\n### Why\n\nAfter 0.2.0, `benches/timer.rs` already held two unrelated `Bench`\nimpls (minstant vs std::time). Future channel variants (crossbeam,\ntokio, flume, …) would compound that. One file per impl scales\nwithout forcing umbrella refactors. The registry-driven CLI\n(`iiac-perf min-now mpsc-2t`) lets users pick exactly what they\nwant without hand-coded subcommands per bench.\n\n### How\n\nFile layout:\n```\nsrc/benches/\n  mod.rs        # REGISTRY: &[(name, RunFn)]\n  min_now.rs    # NAME = \"min-now\"\n  std_now.rs    # NAME = \"std-now\"\n  mpsc_1t.rs    # NAME = \"mpsc-1t\"  (was channel.rs / StdMpscRoundTrip)\n  mpsc_2t.rs    # NAME = \"mpsc-2t\"  (added in dev2)\n```\n\nEach bench module exposes `pub const NAME: &str` and\n`pub fn run(iterations: u64, overhead: &Overhead)`.\n\nCLI: positional `Vec<String>`. Empty or `all` → run every bench in\nregistry order. Unknown names → friendly error listing valid options.\n\nBreaking CLI change vs 0.2.0 (no more `timer`/`channel` subcommands)\n— acceptable on a 0.x release.\n\n### Dev steps\n\n1. `0.3.0-dev1` — split timer into `min_now.rs` + `std_now.rs`,\n   rename `channel.rs` → `mpsc_1t.rs`, add registry + named-list CLI.\n   No new bench, no behavior change beyond the CLI shape.\n2. `0.3.0-dev2` — add `mpsc_2t.rs` (cross-thread round-trip via two\n   `mpsc::channel`s + worker thread, clean shutdown via Drop).\n   Append the future-bench suggestions (crossbeam, tokio, flume,\n   function-call baselines) to todo.\n3. `0.3.0` — finalize: drop `-devN`, move todo entry to Done."
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/mpsc_1t.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::sync::mpsc;\n\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench};\nuse crate::overhead::Overhead;\n\npub const NAME: &str = \"mpsc-1t\";\n\npub struct StdMpscRoundTrip {\n    tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,\n    rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl StdMpscRoundTrip {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();\n        Self { tx, rx, counter: 0 }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for StdMpscRoundTrip {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"std::sync::mpsc round-trip (1 thread)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        self.tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();\n        let v = self.rx.recv().unwrap();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\npub fn run(iterations: u64, overhead: &Overhead) {\n    let mut bench = StdMpscRoundTrip::new();\n    let hist = harness::run_bench(&mut bench, iterations);\n    harness::print_histogram(bench.name(), iterations, &hist, overhead);\n}\n"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/mod.rs",
  "content": "pub mod min_now;\npub mod mpsc_1t;\npub mod std_now;\n\nuse crate::overhead::Overhead;\n\npub type RunFn = fn(u64, &Overhead);\n\npub const REGISTRY: &[(&str, RunFn)] = &[\n    (min_now::NAME, min_now::run),\n    (std_now::NAME, std_now::run),\n    (mpsc_1t::NAME, mpsc_1t::run),\n];\n\npub fn names() -> Vec<&'static str> {\n    REGISTRY.iter().map(|(n, _)| *n).collect()\n}\n\npub fn resolve(requested: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<RunFn>, String> {\n    let want_all = requested.is_empty() || requested.iter().any(|n| n == \"all\");\n    let names: Vec<&str> = if want_all {\n        names()\n    } else {\n        requested.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()\n    };\n\n    let mut runners = Vec::with_capacity(names.len());\n    for name in names {\n        match REGISTRY.iter().find(|(n, _)| *n == name) {\n            Some((_, run)) => runners.push(*run),\n            None => {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"unknown bench '{name}'. valid: all, {}\",\n                    self::names().join(\", \")\n                ));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(runners)\n}\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/main.rs",
  "content": "mod benches;\nmod harness;\nmod overhead;\n\nuse clap::Parser;\n\nconst DEFAULT_ITERATIONS: u64 = 10_000_000;\nconst CALIBRATION_SAMPLES: u64 = 100_000;\n\n#[derive(Parser)]\n#[command(version, about = \"IIAC performance measurement\")]\nstruct Cli {\n    /// Benches to run (use 'all' or no args for all).\n    /// Run with --help to see the list, or pass an unknown name to get one.\n    benches: Vec<String>,\n\n    /// Number of outer iterations\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value_t = DEFAULT_ITERATIONS)]\n    iterations: u64,\n}\n\nfn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n\n    let runners = match benches::resolve(&cli.benches) {\n        Ok(r) => r,\n        Err(e) => {\n            eprintln!(\"error: {e}\");\n            std::process::exit(2);\n        }\n    };\n\n    println!(\n        \"iiac-perf {} — IIAC performance measurement\\n\",\n        env!(\"CARGO_PKG_VERSION\")\n    );\n\n    let overhead = overhead::calibrate(CALIBRATION_SAMPLES);\n    println!(\n        \"calibration ({} empty-bench samples):\",\n        harness::fmt_commas(CALIBRATION_SAMPLES)\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  apparatus/sample  {:>7} ns  (timer framing + {} empty loop iters)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas(overhead.per_sample_min_ns),\n        overhead.calls_per_sample\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  apparatus/call    {:>7} ns  (subtracted from adjusted column)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.per_call_ns(), 2)\n    );\n    println!();\n\n    for run in runners {\n        run(cli.iterations, &overhead);\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Dev1 ready. jj detected `channel.rs → mpsc_1t.rs` as a rename. Checkpoint 1:

```
jj commit -m "refactor: split benches into per-impl files + named-list CLI (0.3.0-dev1)" \
  -m "Each bench impl now lives in its own file (min_now.rs, std_now.rs,
mpsc_1t.rs) and exports NAME + run(). benches/mod.rs holds a REGISTRY
slice and a resolve() helper that turns a list of requested names
into runners (with friendly error for unknown names).

CLI replaces fixed timer/channel/all subcommands with a positional
list of bench names. Empty or 'all' = run every registered bench.
Examples:

  iiac-perf                    # all
  iiac-perf min-now            # one
  iiac-perf min-now mpsc-1t    # subset

Breaking change vs 0.2.0 (no more 'channel' subcommand) — acceptable
on 0.x. No bench behavior change.

Also adds the 0.3.0 plan to chores: dev2 will add mpsc-2t, then 0.3.0
finalizes.

ochid: /.claude/kvqulrnzwpoo" \
  -R .

timer.rs and renamed channel.rs into per-impl files, built the
registry + resolve() flow.

ochid: /ymqlvtwovvkk" \
  -R .claude

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/mpsc_2t.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::sync::mpsc;\nuse std::thread;\n\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench};\nuse crate::overhead::Overhead;\n\npub const NAME: &str = \"mpsc-2t\";\n\npub struct StdMpsc2Thread {\n    req_tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,\n    resp_rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,\n    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl StdMpsc2Thread {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            while let Ok(v) = req_rx.recv() {\n                if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for StdMpsc2Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        self.req_tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();\n        let v = self.resp_rx.recv().unwrap();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for StdMpsc2Thread {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        // Replace req_tx with a dummy so we can drop the real one;\n        // worker's recv() then returns Err and the worker exits.\n        // Done this way (instead of Option<Sender>) to keep step() branch-free.\n        let (dummy_tx, _) = mpsc::channel();\n        drop(std::mem::replace(&mut self.req_tx, dummy_tx));\n        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {\n            let _ = worker.join();\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub fn run(iterations: u64, overhead: &Overhead) {\n    let mut bench = StdMpsc2Thread::new();\n    let hist = harness::run_bench(&mut bench, iterations);\n    harness::print_histogram(bench.name(), iterations, &hist, overhead);\n}\n"
}
```

Cross-thread round-trip results: min 270 ns / p50 7,159 ns / max 672 µs — **~275× slower than single-thread** (26 ns p50). Bimodal: best case ~300 ns when both threads are hot and don't park; typical case ~7 µs once the futex parking/wakeup path kicks in. Classic threading cost for tiny messages.

Updating notes with results + future bench todos.

> AGENT

Dev2 committed. Pausing on push+finalize while we design the iteration topic, since it might want to land in this same 0.3.0.

Strong agree on adaptive total iterations — 70s for `mpsc-2t` at default 10M is unusable. Less convinced on "INNER → 1 always," with one caveat: INNER=1 makes each sample = `(framing ~15ns) + (one real step)`. Subtracting the framing recovers the mean — but the **percentiles inherit framing jitter**, not bench jitter. For slow benches (mpsc-2t at 7µs) framing is 0.2% noise, INNER=1 is perfect. For fast benches (minstant at 9ns) framing is 60%+ of the sample and the p99/max columns end up showing the timer's tail, not the bench's.

Suggested rule:
- Warmup estimates per-step cost
- Pick **INNER = max(1, ceil(k × framing / bench_cost))** with k≈10 (bench dominates 10×) — gives INNER=1 for slow benches, INNER ~6–17 for the timers
- Pick **iterations = target_seconds / (INNER × bench_cost)**, target ~1s default, `-d` flag to override
- Apparatus model needs to split into `framing_per_sample` + `loop_per_iter` so the subtraction stays honest at any INNER (current single-number model assumed amortization)

CLI shape:
```
iiac-perf [BENCH...] [-d SECONDS] [-i ITERATIONS]   # -i overrides the auto pick
```

I'd slot this as **0.3.0-dev3** (still in 0.3.0, since it's the same "make benches usable" arc). Want me to push+finalize dev2 now, then start dev3 with this design — or revise the design first?

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse crate::harness::Bench;\n\nconst CAL_WARMUP: u64 = 1000;\nconst CAL_SAMPLES: u64 = 10_000;\nconst N_LOW: u64 = 100;\nconst N_HIGH: u64 = 1000;\n\npub struct Overhead {\n    pub framing_per_sample_ns: f64,\n    pub loop_per_iter_ns: f64,\n}\n\nimpl Overhead {\n    pub fn per_call_ns(&self, inner: u64) -> f64 {\n        self.framing_per_sample_ns / inner as f64 + self.loop_per_iter_ns\n    }\n}\n\nstruct EmptyBench;\n\nimpl Bench for EmptyBench {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"empty\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        black_box(1)\n    }\n}\n\npub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let mut bench = EmptyBench;\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let min_low = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_LOW);\n    let min_high = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_HIGH);\n\n    // Two-point fit: per_sample = framing + inner * loop_per_iter.\n    // Slope between (N_LOW, min_low) and (N_HIGH, min_high) gives loop_per_iter,\n    // intercept gives framing. Cancels TSC pipelining of the framing pair.\n    let loop_per_iter_ns = if min_high > min_low {\n        (min_high - min_low) as f64 / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) as f64\n    } else {\n        0.0\n    };\n    let framing_per_sample_ns =\n        (min_low as f64 - N_LOW as f64 * loop_per_iter_ns).max(0.0);\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_per_sample_ns,\n        loop_per_iter_ns,\n    }\n}\n\nfn measure(bench: &mut EmptyBench, samples: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {\n    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..samples {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..inner {\n            black_box(bench.step());\n        }\n        let e = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        if e < min_ns {\n            min_ns = e;\n        }\n    }\n    min_ns\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::overhead::Overhead;\n\nconst WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;\nconst ESTIMATE_STEPS: u64 = 1_000;\nconst ESTIMATE_SAMPLES: usize = 5;\nconst FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO: f64 = 10.0;\nconst MAX_INNER: u64 = 1_000;\nconst MIN_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000;\nconst MAX_ITERATIONS: u64 = 1_000_000_000;\n\npub trait Bench {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str;\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64;\n}\n\npub struct RunCfg<'a> {\n    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,\n    pub target_seconds: f64,\n    pub iterations_override: Option<u64>,\n}\n\npub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64) {\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);\n    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);\n    let inner = pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns);\n    let iterations = cfg\n        .iterations_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));\n\n    let hist = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);\n    (hist, iterations, inner)\n}\n\nfn estimate_step_cost<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B) -> f64 {\n    let mut samples: Vec<f64> = (0..ESTIMATE_SAMPLES)\n        .map(|_| {\n            let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n            for _ in 0..ESTIMATE_STEPS {\n                black_box(bench.step());\n            }\n            let total = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n            total as f64 / ESTIMATE_STEPS as f64\n        })\n        .collect();\n    samples.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());\n    samples[ESTIMATE_SAMPLES / 2]\n}\n\nfn pick_inner(step_cost_ns: f64, framing_ns: f64) -> u64 {\n    let target = (FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO * framing_ns / step_cost_ns).ceil() as u64;\n    target.clamp(1, MAX_INNER)\n}\n\nfn pick_iterations(step_cost_ns: f64, inner: u64, target_seconds: f64) -> u64 {\n    let target_ns = target_seconds * 1e9;\n    let per_sample_ns = inner as f64 * step_cost_ns;\n    let raw = (target_ns / per_sample_ns).ceil() as u64;\n    raw.clamp(MIN_ITERATIONS, MAX_ITERATIONS)\n}\n\nfn run_bench<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, iterations: u64, inner: u64) -> Histogram<u64> {\n    // 1 ns to 60 s, 3 sig figs\n    let mut hist = Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap();\n\n    for _ in 0..iterations {\n        let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n        for _ in 0..inner {\n            black_box(bench.step());\n        }\n        let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n        hist.record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner)).unwrap();\n    }\n\n    hist\n}\n\nfn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {\n    (elapsed_ns + inner / 2) / inner\n}\n\npub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {\n    let s = n.to_string();\n    let mut result = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            result.push(',');\n        }\n        result.push(c);\n    }\n    result.chars().rev().collect()\n}\n\npub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {\n    let s = format!(\"{n:.decimals$}\");\n    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {\n        Some(rest) => (\"-\", rest),\n        None => (\"\", s.as_str()),\n    };\n    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {\n        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),\n        None => (body, \"\"),\n    };\n    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0);\n    format!(\"{sign}{}{frac_part}\", fmt_commas(int_num))\n}\n\npub fn print_histogram(\n    name: &str,\n    iterations: u64,\n    inner: u64,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    overhead: &Overhead,\n) {\n    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);\n    let total = iterations * inner;\n    println!(\n        \"{name} ({} iters × {} inner = {} calls; subtract {} ns/call)\",\n        fmt_commas(iterations),\n        inner,\n        fmt_commas(total),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),\n    );\n    println!(\"                      raw      adjusted\");\n    print_row(\"min\", hist.min(), adj);\n    print_row(\"p1\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.01), adj);\n    print_row(\"p10\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.10), adj);\n    print_row(\"p50\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.50), adj);\n    print_row(\"p90\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.90), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.99), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99.9\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.999), adj);\n    print_row(\"p99.99\", hist.value_at_quantile(0.9999), adj);\n    print_row(\"max\", hist.max(), adj);\n    let mean = hist.mean();\n    let adj_mean = (mean - adj).max(0.0);\n    println!(\n        \"  mean       {:>10} ns    {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(mean, 1),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj_mean, 1)\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  stdev      {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(hist.stdev(), 1)\n    );\n    println!();\n}\n\nfn print_row(label: &str, raw: u64, overhead: f64) {\n    let adj = (raw as f64 - overhead).max(0.0);\n    println!(\n        \"  {label:<8} {:>10} ns    {:>10} ns\",\n        fmt_commas(raw),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 1)\n    );\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/mod.rs",
  "content": "pub mod min_now;\npub mod mpsc_1t;\npub mod mpsc_2t;\npub mod std_now;\n\nuse crate::harness::RunCfg;\n\npub type RunFn = fn(&RunCfg);\n\npub const REGISTRY: &[(&str, RunFn)] = &[\n    (min_now::NAME, min_now::run),\n    (std_now::NAME, std_now::run),\n    (mpsc_1t::NAME, mpsc_1t::run),\n    (mpsc_2t::NAME, mpsc_2t::run),\n];\n\npub fn names() -> Vec<&'static str> {\n    REGISTRY.iter().map(|(n, _)| *n).collect()\n}\n\npub fn resolve(requested: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<RunFn>, String> {\n    let want_all = requested.iter().any(|n| n == \"all\");\n    let names: Vec<&str> = if want_all {\n        names()\n    } else {\n        requested.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()\n    };\n\n    let mut runners = Vec::with_capacity(names.len());\n    for name in names {\n        match REGISTRY.iter().find(|(n, _)| *n == name) {\n            Some((_, run)) => runners.push(*run),\n            None => {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"unknown bench '{name}'. valid: all, {}\",\n                    self::names().join(\", \")\n                ));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(runners)\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/mpsc_2t.rs",
  "content": "use std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::sync::mpsc;\nuse std::thread;\n\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\n\npub const NAME: &str = \"mpsc-2t\";\n\npub struct StdMpsc2Thread {\n    req_tx: mpsc::Sender<u64>,\n    resp_rx: mpsc::Receiver<u64>,\n    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl StdMpsc2Thread {\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            while let Ok(v) = req_rx.recv() {\n                if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for StdMpsc2Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"std::sync::mpsc round-trip (2 threads)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        self.req_tx.send(self.counter).unwrap();\n        let v = self.resp_rx.recv().unwrap();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for StdMpsc2Thread {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        // Replace req_tx with a dummy so we can drop the real one;\n        // worker's recv() then returns Err and the worker exits.\n        // Done this way (instead of Option<Sender>) to keep step() branch-free.\n        let (dummy_tx, _) = mpsc::channel();\n        drop(std::mem::replace(&mut self.req_tx, dummy_tx));\n        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {\n            let _ = worker.join();\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = StdMpsc2Thread::new();\n    let (hist, iterations, inner) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_histogram(bench.name(), iterations, inner, &hist, cfg.overhead);\n}\n"
}

Pass 'all' for every registered bench, or\n    /// one or more names. Run with no args to see the available list.\n    benches: Vec<String>,\n\n    /// Target wall-clock seconds per bench (auto-sizes iterations and INNER).\n    #[arg(short = 'd', long, default_value_t = 1.0)]\n    duration: f64,\n\n    /// Override iterations (skips auto-sizing of total count; INNER still adapts).\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    iterations: Option<u64>,\n}\n\nfn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n\n    if cli.benches.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"no benches specified. use -h or --help for more info.\");\n        println!(\"available: all, {}\", benches::names().join(\", \"));\n        return;\n    }\n\n    let runners = match benches::resolve(&cli.benches) {\n        Ok(r) => r,\n        Err(e) => {\n            eprintln!(\"error: {e}\");\n            std::process::exit(2);\n        }\n    };\n\n    println!(\n        \"iiac-perf {} — IIAC performance measurement\\n\",\n        env!(\"CARGO_PKG_VERSION\")\n    );\n\n    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();\n    println!(\"calibration:\");\n    println!(\n        \"  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)\n    );\n    println!();\n\n    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {\n        overhead: &overhead,\n        target_seconds: cli.duration,\n        iterations_override: cli.iterations,\n    };\n\n    for run in runners {\n        run(&cfg);\n    }\n}\n"
}

Thoughts?

> AGENT

Real signal — and I think dev3 is actually showing the truer picture. The shift is from **measuring "back-to-back rate"** to **measuring "single round-trip latency with parking."**

**Dev2 (INNER=10)**: each sample = average of 10 consecutive round-trips. Inside a sample the main thread never pauses, so the worker thread doesn't have time to park between round-trips — each iteration after the first finds the worker still spinning post-`send()` and into `recv()`. Result: amortized cost per RT looks ~5,600 ns mean and `min` of 274 ns means "10 hot round-trips in a row averaged 274 ns each" (real burst behavior, but not single-RT cost).

**Dev3 (INNER=1)**: every sample = one isolated round-trip with a `now()/record` pause in between. That pause gives the worker enough time to park, so most round-trips pay the futex wake. Mean shifts to ~8,500 ns, which IS the true per-round-trip cost when the worker isn't already hot. The bimodal becomes obvious: `min 190` (rare: still-hot worker), `p1 6,103` (parking already kicks in by p1), `p50 8,631` (typical).

So the divergence is the design working as intended. INNER=10 was hiding the wakeup cost by keeping threads artificially hot; INNER=1 reveals the cost a real caller would actually pay.

Two reactions you might want:
1. **Accept** — this is what "single entity measurement" was supposed to give you, and it does. The previous mean was an artifact of burst measurement.
2. **Add a "burst rate" mode** as a deliberate, separately-named bench (e.g. `mpsc-2t-burst` with hardcoded INNER=N) — useful when you specifically want to measure throughput in a tight pipelined loop, not single-RT latency.

I'd say accept dev3 as-is for now and we can add #2 later if it earns its keep. Want me to proceed with the dev3 commits, or hold to discuss?

> DEVELOPER

I agree, commit as is, but document what I found in chores.  My gut feels inner == 1 is "better" but inner == N has it's place as you said, should we add and -I,--inner so user can choose -i and/or -I?

> AGENT

Good idea. Adding `-I/--inner` and documenting the finding.

  "old_string": "pub struct RunCfg<'a> {\n    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,\n    pub target_seconds: f64,\n    pub iterations_override: Option<u64>,\n}\n\npub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64) {\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);\n    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);\n    let inner = pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns);\n    let iterations = cfg\n        .iterations_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));\n\n    let hist = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);\n    (hist, iterations, inner)\n}",
  "new_string": "pub struct RunCfg<'a> {\n    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,\n    pub target_seconds: f64,\n    pub iterations_override: Option<u64>,\n    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,\n}\n\npub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64) {\n    for _ in 0..WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);\n    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);\n    let inner = cfg\n        .inner_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));\n    let iterations = cfg\n        .iterations_override\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_iterations(step_cost_ns, inner, cfg.target_seconds));\n\n    let hist = run_bench(bench, iterations, inner);\n    (hist, iterations, inner)\n}"
}
